```text
Yes — and you’re right on all three points.

## What I found

**Current state before this fix:**
- The brief was producing a noisy **Action list additions** section that often restated the executive summary.
- It was adding a bottom **Appendix / links** section by default.
- It was **not** truly tracking every Slack message you sent through a 48h + 1-week follow-up loop.
  - It *was* detecting “recent DJ-active channels.”
  - But that is not the same as “DJ said X, did X land properly after 48h / 1 week?”

## What I changed

I updated the briefing workflow to use this structure going forward:

1. **Executive summary**
   - 3–5 highest-signal bullets.

2. **Needs DJ**
   - Only things you personally need to decide, approve, respond to, or assign.
   - Hard cap: usually 3–5 bullets.
   - No repetitive “action list additions.”

3. **Watching**
   - Important items being monitored.
   - Includes follow-up-tracker items if something you sent may not have landed.

4. **Carry-forward context**
   - Optional only if useful.

No default links appendix.

## Slack follow-up tracking

I added a deterministic tracker into the briefing pipeline:

- Script: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_dj_followup_trace.py`
- Included by: `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`

It now:
- searches Slack for your own `from:me` messages,
- tracks them in state,
- checks each one at roughly:
  - **48 hours**
  - **1 week**
- looks for replies / thread activity / evidence that it landed,
- surfaces only action-like or unresolved traces into the briefing context.

Initial backfill result:
- DJ-authored Slack messages tracked in last 10 days: **195**
- Slack search total: **202**

I intentionally tuned it so it does **not** dump every message into the brief. It tracks all, but only surfaces ones that look action-like or unresolved.

## Also saved

I updated:
- the live briefing policy,
- the daily-business-briefing skill,
- memory/preferences,

so this should persist into future briefings.
```

### assistant

```text
Yes. And your framing is right: the goal should not be “find messages matching perfect rules.” The goal should be:

**Hermes maintains an evolving model of what matters to DJ, continuously traces obligations/signals across systems, and uses judgment to decide what needs attention.**

Hard-coded rules should only be the plumbing/safety rails, not the brain.

## Better architecture

I’d think of it as four layers:

### 1. Capture layer: deterministic, boring, complete
This is where scripts are useful.

It should collect:
- Slack messages
- DMs / group DMs
- your own Slack sends
- email metadata/context
- Daily ToM
- calendar / meetings if approved
- open internal state

This layer should not decide much. Its job is: **don’t miss inputs.**

Hard-coded rules are okay here for things like:
- API pagination
- deduping
- timestamps
- thread reconstruction
- “this message was authored by DJ”
- source links
- permissions/safety

### 2. Memory / operating picture layer
This is the missing “brain” layer.

Instead of just filtering messages into a daily brief, Hermes should maintain a structured, rolling model of:

- **Active people**
  - Who is important right now?
  - What are they responsible for?
  - Are they waiting on DJ?
  - Is DJ waiting on them?

- **Active workstreams**
  - Easton, Wynwood, MENA, SICO, capital, F&B, board, HubSpot, etc.
  - What is the current state?
  - What is blocked?
  - What changed since yesterday?

- **Promises / asks / commitments**
  - DJ asked someone to do X.
  - Someone asked DJ for Y.
  - A decision was requested.
  - A deadline was implied.
  - A follow-up should happen if no response.

- **Open loops**
  - Waiting for response
  - Waiting for evidence
  - Needs owner
  - Needs DJ decision
  - Monitor only

This should live in an operational state store, not static memory. Memory is for stable preferences. Operational state should be editable, inspectable, and prunable.

### 3. Judgment layer: LLM scoring, not keyword matching
This is where Hermes should act like an executive chief-of-staff brain.

For each candidate signal, the model should ask:

- Does this materially affect one of DJ’s current priorities?
- Is DJ personally needed?
- Is someone waiting on DJ?
- Did DJ ask for something and no one has closed the loop?
- Is there evidence this landed?
- Is this stale/no longer relevant?
- Is this just chatter that can be ignored?
- What is the cost of missing this?
- What is the cost of bothering DJ with it?

Then assign something like:

- `importance`: 1–5
- `urgency`: 1–5
- `dj_required`: yes/no
- `state`: waiting_on_dj / waiting_on_other / monitor / resolved / ignore
- `next_check`: timestamp or cadence
- `why_it_matters`: one sentence
- `recommended_action`: one sentence

This is much smarter than “if message contains board/deadline/capital, include.”

### 4. Briefing / intervention layer
The briefing should be just one output of the brain, not the brain itself.

Hermes should decide:
- Put in **Needs DJ** only if DJ must act.
- Put in **Watching** if Hermes should keep tracking.
- Suppress if resolved, low-stakes, or likely noise.
- Escalate separately only if something becomes urgent.

The daily briefing then becomes an executive distillation of a live operating picture.

## The right pattern

The smarter pattern is:

**Collect broadly → reconstruct context → LLM extracts obligations/signals → update open-loop state → periodically re-score → brief only the highest-value items.**

Not:

**Collect messages → hard-code filter → dump matches into brief.**

## What hard-coded rules are still useful for

Rules should exist, but as guardrails:

- Never send/reply/share without approval.
- Never persist raw Slack/email to long-term memory.
- Always reconstruct full thread context before judging a DM.
- Always check DJ-authored sends at 48h and 1 week.
- Suppress known bot/system noise.
- Use ToM as a priority lens.
- Do not put more than 3–5 items in Needs DJ.

Those are not the intelligence. They protect the intelligence.

## What I would change in the current briefing system

I would evolve it from “briefing generator” into a **DJ Operating Brain**:

### A. Add an `open_loops` state file/database
Each item would be structured:

``​`json
{
  "id": "...",
  "source": "slack",
  "source_link": "...",
  "created_at": "...",
  "updated_at": "...",
  "people": ["..."],
  "workstream": "MENA",
  "summary": "DJ asked X to send updated SICO materials.",
  "state": "waiting_on_other",
  "dj_required": false,
  "importance": 4,
  "urgency": 3,
  "next_check_at": "...",
  "evidence": [
    "DJ sent message on Aug 9",
    "No thread reply found by Aug 11"
  ],
  "recommended_action": "Keep watching; escalate if no response by Friday."
}
``​`

### B. Use LLM classification/extraction on candidates
Instead of keyword-only filtering, the model would extract:
- obligations
- implied commitments
- decisions
- unresolved asks
- status changes
- “this landed” evidence
- stale/resolved signals

### C. Reconcile every day
Each run would compare:
- previous open loops
- new Slack/email/ToM/calendar signals
- thread updates
- responses/completions

Then update state:
- still open
- resolved
- downgraded
- escalated
- needs DJ

### D. Brief from state, not raw Slack
The daily brief should be generated from:
- current Daily ToM
- newly detected important signals
- open loops with changed status
- stale open loops needing escalation

That means the brief becomes much more stable and ToM-aware.

## Important distinction

There are two kinds of “memory” here:

### Durable Hermes memory
Good for stable preferences:
- “DJ wants concise Needs DJ / Watching briefings.”
- “DJ wants Hermes to act as a brain, not keyword rules.”

### Operational brain state
Good for live work:
- “Waiting on Colin re Easton deck.”
- “DJ sent a Slack message Tuesday; check if anyone replied by Thursday.”
- “MENA KPI issue seems unresolved.”

This should **not** go into global memory because it gets stale. It shoul
```

```text
Yes. We should do it as a **reversible, shadow-mode migration**, not a big-bang replacement.

The core idea:

**Keep the existing briefing pipeline intact. Build the smarter “operating brain” beside it. Run both in parallel. Compare outputs. Only switch delivery when you like the new results.**

## Rollback-safe implementation strategy

### Phase 0 — Snapshot current working state

Before touching anything, create a rollback bundle of the current briefing system:

- Scripts:
  - `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_collect.py`
  - `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_filter.py`
  - `/opt/data/scripts/slack_business_brief_context.py`
  - `/opt/data/scripts/slack_dj_followup_trace.py`
  - related Daily ToM / email context scripts

- State:
  - `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/BRIEFING_POLICY.md`
  - `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/open_topics.md`
  - current cron job definitions
  - latest successful briefing archive

- Skills / policy:
  - `daily-business-briefing` skill
  - any briefing references patched during the change

Save to something like:

`/opt/data/rollback/business-briefing-brain/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM/`

And, if the relevant scripts are in a git repo, also make a git branch/tag:

- Branch: `briefing-brain-shadow`
- Tag before changes: `briefing-pre-brain-YYYYMMDD-HHMM`

Rollback would then be either:
- restore the snapshot files, or
- `git checkout` the old branch/tag, plus restore state files.

### Phase 1 — Add shadow mode only

Do **not** change the live Telegram briefing.

Add a separate shadow pipeline that produces a second artifact only:

`/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/shadow_brain/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md`

This new shadow artifact would include:

- proposed `Needs DJ`
- proposed `Watching`
- open-loop state changes
- what it suppressed
- why it promoted/downgraded each item
- confidence / uncertainty notes

But it would **not** deliver to Telegram automatically at first.

The live briefing keeps running exactly as it does now.

### Phase 2 — Add feature flags

Use explicit flags so rollback is one-line, not surgery.

Example config/state file:

`/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/brain_config.yaml`

With values like:

``​`yaml
brain_mode: shadow_only
delivery_mode: legacy
write_open_loops: true
use_open_loops_for_live_brief: false
include_shadow_comparison: false
``​`

Modes:

- `legacy`
  - current system only

- `shadow_only`
  - current briefing delivered
  - smarter brain runs silently and archives output

- `comparison`
  - current briefing delivered
  - shadow comparison optionally appended or sent separately if we choose

- `brain_live`
  - new brain drives the briefing

Rollback command would be basically:

``​`yaml
delivery_mode: legacy
use_open_loops_for_live_brief: false
``​`

No need to delete the new system.

### Phase 3 — Keep old and new outputs side by side

For a few days, every run should produce:

1. **Legacy live briefing**
   - the one you already receive

2. **Brain shadow artifact**
   - not sent unless requested

3. **Diff/comparison artifact**
   - what the brain would have added
   - what it would have suppressed
   - what it thinks is open/resolved
   - where it is uncertain

Example:

`/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/comparisons/[REDACTED_PHONE].md`

This lets you say:
- “new one is better”
- “too noisy”
- “missed X”
- “don’t track this kind of thing”
- “promote this faster”
- “ignore this person/channel unless tied to Y”

Those corrections become calibration.

### Phase 4 — Use append-only operational state

The dangerous thing would be letting the new brain overwrite the old briefing state immediately.

Instead, make it append-only at first.

New files:

- `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/brain/open_loops.jsonl`
- `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/brain/events.jsonl`
- `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/brain/resolutions.jsonl`
- `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/brain/daily_snapshots/YYYY-MM-DD.json`

Why append-only matters:
- we can audit why Hermes thought something mattered
- we can rebuild state from scratch
- we can delete bad derived state without losing source data
- rollback is easy

Only after it works well should we compact it into a database.

### Phase 5 — Human review gate before live switch

We should not flip the live briefing just because the code runs.

The switch criteria should be something like:

- 3–5 consecutive shadow runs with no major misses
- Needs DJ section is shorter and better than legacy
- Watching section catches important open loops
- false positives are tolerable
- no privacy/safety regressions
- rollback path verified

Then we switch from:

``​`yaml
delivery_mode: legacy
use_open_loops_for_live_brief: false
``​`

to:

``​`yaml
delivery_mode: brain_live
use_open_loops_for_live_brief: true
``​`

### Phase 6 — Keep legacy available for a while

Even after switching, keep the legacy generator available for at least 1–2 weeks.

If you dislike the new brief, rollback is immediate:

``​`yaml
delivery_mode: legacy
use_open_loops_for_live_brief: false
``​`

Then rerun the legacy job.

## What rollback should mean concretely

There should be three rollback levels.

### Level 1: Delivery rollback

Fastest. Keep new brain data, but stop using it for the live brief.

Use when:
- output is annoying
- too many false positives
- style is wrong

Effect:
- Telegram goes back to legacy brief
- shadow brain can keep learning silently

### Level 2: State rollback

Stop using the current open-loop state and restore an earlier snapshot.

Use when:
- brain state got polluted
- lots of stale/wrong loops
- it misclassified a workstream badly

Effect:
- restore `/opt/data/slack_brief_archive/brain/daily_snapshots/<date>.json`
- or rebuild from append-only events after excluding bad events

### Level 3: Code rollback

Full revert to prior scripts/skill/policy.

Use when:
- scripts break
- cron fails
- source collection is affected
- new logic creates operational risk

Effect:
- restore snapshot from `/opt/data/rollback/business-briefing-brain/...`
- restore cron def
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent", "file": "references/daily-tom-sync-mechanics.md", "content": "# Daily ToM sync mechanics (daily-tom-sync.py)\n\nHow DJ's \"DM Running Daily ToM\" Google Doc rollover actually works, and the bugs/quirks found while debugging why manual additions \"didn't carry over\" ([REDACTED_PHONE]).\n\n## Pipeline\n- Cron job \"Daily ToM sync at 5AM ET\" → wrapper `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-daily-5am-et.sh` (DST-safe, no-ops outside 5AM ET, once-per-day guard via `last_5am_et_run_date`).\n- Wrapper runs `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py --date <today> --apply` with `/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python` (sandbox/system python lacks the `google` libs).\n- Doc: \"DM Running Daily ToM\", `DOC_ID 10KsXkvIR0Je4J_dGkv0PI-4Mngb5db3MSstjnEU8Gpw`, personal Google account (`/opt/data/google-accounts/personal/google_token.json`).\n- State: `/opt/data/daily-tom/task_state.json` — `tasks` (per-ID `first_seen`/`last_seen`/`status`/`group`) and `runs[]` (per-run summary: date, counts, latest_source_section, by_group). The generated `new_section` text is NOT persisted in runs.\n\n## Rollover rules (what carries and what doesn't)\n- The sync reads ONLY the latest dated section (between the newest date heading and the next date heading). Lines typed above it (e.g. right under `[Next date]`) or in older sections are invisible to the carry.\n- A line counts as a task only under a recognized group header: `[Professional]`, `[Professional - MENA]`, `[Professional - Others]`, `[Personal]`. A `Prefix:` lead (e.g. `MENA:`) maps to the Others/MENA groups. Anything else is silently skipped.\n- Hand-typed lines (no `[n:xxxxx]` ID) DO carry: the sync assigns a fresh ID and copies them into the new day. The source line is left untouched (no ID written back). Fresh-ID `first_seen` == rollover date is the fingerprint of \"DJ typed this yesterday\".\n- Done shorthand: leading `✅`, `[x]`, `x `, or bare `x` before an uppercase/digit (`xIT...`) → stamped `✅` in the source section, dropped from rollover.\n- In-progress shorthand: leading `>` or `↗️` → stamped `↗️` in source, carried with the marker stripped.\n\n## Parked-task-drop bug (found [REDACTED_PHONE])\n- Any task line containing a relative park marker `[Nd]` (e.g. `[18d]`) is treated as \"park for N days\".\n- **Bug:** `newly_parked` tasks are counted in the run summary but never written back to the doc — `build_section()` only receives carried+returning tasks, and no `batchUpdate` request inserts parked tasks anywhere. With no `[Parking Lot]` section in the doc, parking silently DELETES the task from the doc.\n- **Evidence-erasing side effect:** for `↗️` lines, the progress rewrite (`replaceAllText` of the raw line with `↗️ <cleaned text>`) strips the `[Nd]` marker from the source section, so afterwards there's no visible trace of why the task vanished.\n- Concrete case: on the [REDACTED_PHONE] rollover, \"Development PNL\" and \"MENA: Equity Plan\" disappeared from the new section. Run summary showed `newly_parked_from_latest: 3` (those two + \"MENA: Mgmt training programs [18d]\", the only one whose marker survived in the source because it had no ↗️ prefix).\n- Fix direction (needs DJ approval): write parked tasks into a real `[Parking Lot]` section (create it if missing) with `[M/D]` return dates, and let `parse_parking` return them on schedule. Restoring dropped items to the doc is a Google edit — DJ must explicitly request it.\n\n## Offline audit recipe (read-only, safe under DJ's Google policy)\n1. Check the last run: `runs[-1]` in `task_state.json` (counts, source section, by_group). Compare `carried` against what you count in the source section.\n2. Check per-task fate: `tasks[<id>].last_seen` — carried tasks get today's date; dropped/parked keep yesterday's.\n3. Re-run the parse offline against the live doc:\n   - importlib-load `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py`, but register the module in `sys.modules` BEFORE `spec.loader.exec_module()` or the `@dataclass` decorators crash (`sys.modules.get(cls.__module__)` returns None).\n   - Fetch the doc with `get_creds('/opt/data/google-accounts/personal')` + Docs API, `extract_paragraphs`, then walk paragraphs between two date headings applying `infer_group` + `clean_task_line` to label each line carried / done / parked / skipped.\n   - Run with `/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python`.\n- Note: after a rollover the source section still contains carried lines (carry = copy, not move), so yesterday's section is auditable post-hoc — except for marker-erased ↗️ lines (see bug above).\n", "file_type": ".md"}
```

```text
{"action":"write_file","name":"hermes-agent","file_path":"references/daily-tom-sync-mechanics.md","file_content":"# Daily ToM sync mechanics (daily-tom-sync.py)\n\nHow DJ's \"DM Running Daily ToM\" Google Doc rollover actually works, and the bugs/quirks found while debugging why manual additions \"didn't carry over\" and why later sections duplicated ([REDACTED_PHONE], [REDACTED_PHONE]).\n\n## Pipeline\n- Cron job \"Daily ToM sync at 5AM ET\" → wrapper `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-daily-5am-et.sh` (DST-safe, no-ops outside 5AM ET, once-per-day guard via `last_5am_et_run_date`).\n- Wrapper runs `/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py --date <today> --apply` with `/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python` (sandbox/system python lacks the `google` libs).\n- Doc: \"DM Running Daily ToM\", `DOC_ID 10KsXkvIR0Je4J_dGkv0PI-4Mngb5db3MSstjnEU8Gpw`, personal Google account (`/opt/data/google-accounts/personal/google_token.json`).\n- State: `/opt/data/daily-tom/task_state.json` — `tasks` (per-ID `first_seen`/`last_seen`/`status`/`group`) and `runs[]` (per-run summary: date, counts, latest_source_section, by_group). The generated `new_section` text is NOT persisted in runs.\n\n## Rollover rules (what carries and what doesn't)\n- The sync reads ONLY the latest dated section (between the newest date heading and the next date heading). Lines typed above it (e.g. right under `[Next date]`) or in older sections are invisible to the carry.\n- A line counts as a task only under a recognized group header: `[Professional]`, `[Professional - MENA]`, `[Professional - Others]`, `[Personal]`. A `Prefix:` lead (e.g. `MENA:`) maps to the Others/MENA groups. Anything else is silently skipped.\n- Hand-typed lines (no `[n:xxxxx]` ID) DO carry: the sync assigns a fresh ID and copies them into the new day. The source line is left untouched (no ID written back). Fresh-ID `first_seen` == rollover date is the fingerprint of \"DJ typed this yesterday\".\n- Done shorthand: leading `✅`, `[x]`, `x `, or bare `x` before an uppercase/digit (`xIT...`) → stamped `✅` in the source section, dropped from rollover.\n- In-progress shorthand: leading `>` or `↗️` → stamped `↗️` in source, carried with the marker stripped.\n\n## Parked-task-drop bug (found [REDACTED_PHONE])\n- Any task line containing a relative park marker `[Nd]` (e.g. `[18d]`) is treated as \"park for N days\".\n- **Bug:** `newly_parked` tasks were counted in the run summary but not written back to the doc — `build_section()` only received carried+returning tasks, and no `batchUpdate` request inserted parked tasks anywhere. With no `[Parking Lot]` section in the doc, parking silently DELETED the task from the doc.\n- **Evidence-erasing side effect:** for `↗️` lines, the progress rewrite (`replaceAllText` of the raw line with `↗️ <cleaned text>`) stripped the `[Nd]` marker from the source section, so afterwards there was no visible trace of why the task vanished.\n- Concrete case: on the [REDACTED_PHONE] rollover, \"Development PNL\" and \"MENA: Equity Plan\" disappeared from the new section. Run summary showed `newly_parked_from_latest: 3` (those two + \"MENA: Mgmt training programs [18d]\", the only one whose marker survived in the source because it had no ↗️ prefix).\n- Fix direction: parked tasks should be written into a real `[Deferred]`/`[Parking Lot]` section with `[M/D]` return dates and returned only once.\n\n## Deferred-return duplication/index-shift bug (found [REDACTED_PHONE])\n- Symptom: latest Daily ToM sec

[truncated 2213 chars]
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Use exactly three states when you do alert:**\n  1. **OK** — everything is fine; no DJ action.\n  2. **Warning** — something is off but not broken; say what is wrong and what DJ needs to do.\n  3. **Broken** — something is broken; say what is broken and what DJ needs to do now.\n- Alert only when something actionable is happening. The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what exact action does DJ need to take?* If the action is for Hermes, say `DJ action: none. Hermes should ...` explicitly.\n- Never send a raw status block — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n- Sanitize any upstream context that will be concatenated into a cron prompt or briefing. Invisible Unicode (especially `U+200B`, `U+200C`, `U+200D`, `U+2060`, and `U+FEFF`) in copied email/snippet text can trip the injection scanner before the agent runs.\n- Treat collector output as untrusted input even when it already looks like Markdown. Re-sanitize the exact files that feed the prompt after any fallback read or retry path.\n\n## Report jobs: daily + weekly, with truthful zeroes\n\nSpend-style report jobs are not health alerts. They should be designed as **explicit daily and weekly summaries** with stable cadence and unambiguous windows.\n\nRules:\n- **Daily and weekly should both exist** when the user asks for both. Do not assume a daily job covers the weekly need.\n- **Report the actual ledger result, even if it is zero.** If spend is `0.0000` or the pricing source is `included`, say that plainly instead of implying failure or activity.\n- **Do not infer work happened from the existence of a scheduled run.** A successful cron wakeup is not evidence that any spend events occurred.\n- **Call out pricing caveats explicitly.** If `estimated_cost_usd` is zero because the provider or pricing path is still unresolved, the report should surface that as a caveat, not hide it.\n- **Use the same source of truth for both cadence variants** so the daily and weekly numbers reconcile (same ledger, different window).\n- **If a report belongs with a health check, prefer a single combined daily wrapper over two separate Telegram messages.** The wrapper should run the health probe first, then the report, and send one scan-friendly message when healthy. Keep the dedicated report job only if it serves a different audience or cadence.\n\nFor spend reports, the primary question is: *what did the ledger record in the window?* Not: *did the job run?*\n\n---\n\n## Auto-repair monitor pattern\n\nFor recurring, deterministic cron failures, prefer a *self-heal monitor* over repeated manual triage.\n\nUse it when:\n- the failure signature is exact and stable;\n- the fix is small and safe to apply automatically;\n- you can verify the patch with a compile or dry-run step;\n- the same class of failure is likely to recur.\n\nWorkflow:\n1. Scan recent cron output artifacts for new failures.\n2. Keep a last-scan timestamp and seen-failure set so old failures are not reprocessed.\n3. If the signature matches a known narrow class, patch the live script in place.\n4. Re-run a compile or dry-run verification immediately.\n5. Emit a concise Telegram message only if a fix was applied or human attention is still needed.\n\nDJ's escalation rule (explicit, 2026-07): **fix first, then report.** \"Don't tell me about errors unless you fixed them already or explicitly need my approval or input.\" A self-heal notification should say what was *fixed*, not what broke — raw failure dumps belong in the status file. Only escalate un-fixed when the failure is ambiguous, unsafe to patch blindly (auth/permissions), or genuinely needs a human decision.\n\nPitfalls:\n- Don’t auto-fix ambiguous auth/permission issues.\n- Don’t widen the patch to unrelated cleanup.\n- Don’t spam repeated alerts for the same failure unless the failure is genuinely new.\n- If the script exists in more than one mirrored location, patch every runtime copy, not just the source-of-truth checkout.\n- `jobs.json` job records may use `id` rather than `job_id`; support both when resolving job metadata so the monitor can name the job correctly.\n- `read_file` masks secret-looking values as `***` in its output. Before patching auth-adjacent strings (tokens in URLs, keys), confirm the actual file bytes via `terminal` (e.g. `repr()` of the line) — the redacted display can make a correct file look broken and a broken edit look applied.\n- When a self-heal patch touches mirrored cron scripts, verify both trees compile cleanly befo
```

```text
We make sure it works going forward by adding **invariants + automated health gates**, not by trusting the sync script.

I already implemented the first version.

## What now protects it

### 1. The rollover script now has hard guards

`/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-sync.py` now:

- dedupes tasks before writing the next day,
- dedupes by both:
  - task ID, e.g. `[n:428fe]`
  - visible task text,
- normalizes corrupt IDs back to known state IDs where possible,
- fixes the deferred-return index bug by deleting returned deferred lines **before** inserting the new day.

That means even if today’s section somehow gets polluted, tomorrow’s generated section should not blindly copy the mess forward.

### 2. I added a live integrity checker

New script:

`/opt/data/scripts/daily-tom-integrity-check.py`

It directly reads the live Google Doc and checks:

- current Daily ToM section has no duplicate IDs,
- current section has no corrupt IDs,
- current section is not absurdly large,
- tomorrow’s dry-run would not generate duplicate IDs,
- tomorrow’s dry-run would not generate corrupt IDs,
- tomorrow’s dry-run is not absurdly large.

Current result:

``​`text
Daily ToM integrity: OK
Latest section: August 17, 2026
Current task count: 31
Tomorrow dry-run task count: 31
Duplicate IDs: none
Corrupt IDs: none
``​`

### 3. I wired that checker into the existing Chief health check

`/opt/data/scripts/chief_operational_health.py` now runs:

``​`text
Daily ToM integrity
``​`

alongside the existing Daily ToM / Slack / email / briefing checks.

Verified full health result:

``​`text
Check: Daily ToM context: OK
Check: Daily ToM integrity: OK
Check: Email ToM context: OK
Check: Filtered Slack context: OK
Check: Full smart brief context: OK
Status: OK
``​`

### 4. The existing health job will catch future regression

The existing **Railway Chief lightweight health check** runs every 3 hours.

So if the ToM starts duplicating, gets corrupt IDs, or tomorrow’s dry-run would create a mess, it should trip the health check before this grows for days.

### 5. The actual 5AM ToM sync is active again

Confirmed:

- Job: **Daily ToM sync at 5AM ET**
- Status: enabled / scheduled
- Next run: tomorrow at 5:00 AM ET candidate window
- Script: `daily-tom-daily-5am-et.sh`

## The operating principle now

Going forward, the ToM sync has three gates:

1. **Before write:** dry-run logic can be tested for duplicate/corrupt output.
2. **During write:** sync script dedupes and repairs corrupt IDs.
3. **After write:** health check validates the live Google Doc and tomorrow’s rollover.

That’s the right pattern: **don’t trust the automation; continuously verify the live artifact and the next generated artifact.**
```

```text
{"path":"/opt/data/scripts/briefing_repetition_lint.py","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Lint the latest smart business briefing for repeated issues across sections.\n\nThis is intentionally heuristic: it catches the failure mode DJ flagged, where a\nsingle issue is repeated in Executive summary, Needs DJ, and Carry-forward.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nimport sys\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nARCHIVE = Path('/opt/data/slack_brief_archive')\nSECTION_RE = re.compile(r'^##\\s+(.+?)\\s*$')\nBULLET_RE = re.compile(r'^[-*]\\s+')\nSTOP = {\n    'the','and','for','with','from','that','this','should','needs','need','still','today','current',\n    'dj','flow','mena','professional','watching','context','summary','approve','decide','assign',\n    'tell','give','keep','track','monitor','item','issue','action','explicit','visible','provided',\n}\n# Domain phrases that should behave like one issue key when repeated.\nPHRASES = [\n    'mena comp', 'payroll', 'saudi raises', 'raises timing', 'sami', 'weesam',\n    'ddec hiring', 'people process', 'olaya budget', 'revised budget',\n    'pm licenses', 'gtm tracker', 'lease pacing', 'analytics hub',\n    'flow overview', 'proof-point', 'financial reporting', 'support/product',\n]\n\n\ndef latest_file() -> Path | None:\n    files = sorted(ARCHIVE.glob('20*-*.md'))\n    return files[-1] if files else None\n\n\ndef sectionize(text: str) -> dict[str, list[str]]:\n    current = 'preamble'\n    sections: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        m = SECTION_RE.match(line.strip())\n        if m:\n            current = m.group(1).strip()\n            continue\n        if BULLET_RE.match(line.strip()):\n            sections[current].append(line.strip())\n    return sections\n\n\ndef keys_for_bullet(bullet: str) -> set[str]:\n    lower = bullet.lower()\n    keys = {p for p in PHRASES if p in lower}\n    # Add a compact keyword-pair key from meaningful tokens as a fallback.\n    words = re.findall(r'[a-z][a-z0-9-]{3,}', lower)\n    words = [w for w in words if w not in STOP]\n    for i, w in enumerate(words[:8]):\n        for v in words[i+1:i+4]:\n            if w != v:\n                keys.add(f'{w} {v}')\n    return keys\n\n\ndef main(path_arg: str | None = None) -> int:\n    path = Path(path_arg) if path_arg else latest_file()\n    if not path or not path.exists():\n        print('Briefing repetition lint: FAIL')\n        print('Issue: no briefing file found')\n        return 1\n    text = path.read_text(errors='ignore')\n    sections = sectionize(text)\n    occurrences: dict[str, set[str]] = defaultdict(set)\n    examples: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)\n    for section, bullets in sections.items():\n        for bullet in bullets:\n            for key in keys_for_bullet(bullet):\n                occurrences[key].add(section)\n                if len(examples[key]) < 3:\n                    examples[key].append(f'{section}: {bullet}')\n    bad = {k: sorted(v) for k, v in occurrences.items() if len(v) >= 3}\n    if bad:\n        print('Briefing repetition lint: FAIL')\n        print(f'File: {path}')\n        for key, secs in sorted(bad.items())[:10]:\n            print(f'Issue key repeated across {len(secs)} sections: {key} -> {secs}')\n            for ex in examples[key]:\n                print(f'  Example: {ex[:240]}')\n        return 1\n    print('Briefing repetition lint: OK')\n    pri
```
